Well, I came in because... I just can’t seem to get anything in order anymore. It’s getting so bad. If you saw my kitchen table right now, you'd be shocked. It is just completely covered with my husband’s appointment papers—he’s been having this awful trouble with his sciatica, and the physical therapist gave him all these stretches to do, but does he do them? No, he just leaves the papers piled on the table. 

And then mixed in with that are all the church flyers, and notes from the senior center... Oh, they’re doing a bake sale next month, which reminds me I really need to find my mother's recipe for lemon bars. She used to make them for every church picnic when I was a girl, back when we lived in Pennsylvania. They had this wonderful powdered sugar crust. 

But anyway, the table is just buried under all that, and all these half-finished to-do lists I keep writing for myself. I pull up a chair and just sit there staring at the mess, not knowing which thing to pick up or do first... and then I look at the clock and the whole day is just gone.

Oh, no, I don't have any trouble throwing things in the trash. I actually like getting rid of things when I know they're garbage. The problem is just sorting through the piles to figure out what's what. But once it's in the bin, it goes right out to the curb on Tuesday mornings. 

Our garbage man, Frank... oh, he is just the sweetest man. He’s been doing our neighborhood route for, gosh, it must be fifteen years now. He used to bring his golden retriever in the cab of the truck with him. Such a beautiful dog, her name was Daisy. I used to keep those little milk-bone biscuits in the pantry just in case I saw them out the window. Though, my goodness, have you seen the price of pet food lately? I was at the grocery store last week and a small box of treats was almost seven dollars. I just couldn't believe it. Everything is getting so terribly expensive, especially eggs. My husband loves his eggs over easy every morning, but at these prices...

throwing things in the trash. I actually like getting rid of things when I know they're garbage. The problem is just sorting through the piles to figure out what's what. But once it's in the bin, it goes right out to the curb on Tuesday mornings. \n\nOur garbage man, Frank... oh, he is just the sweetest man. He’s been doing our neighborhood route for, gosh, it must be fifteen years now. He used to bring his golden retriever in the cab of the truck with him. Such a beautiful dog, her name was Daisy. I used to keep those little milk-bone biscuits in the pantry just in case I saw them out the window. Though, my goodness, have you seen the price of pet food lately? I was at the grocery store last week and a small box of treats was almost seven dollars. I just couldn't believe it. Everything is getting so terribly expensive, especially eggs. My husband loves his eggs over easy every morning, but at these prices... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, throwing things away. No, no, I don't hold onto useless things or anything like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been this easily distracted and disorganized since you were young?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, yes. It feels like it's been this way forever. Even when I was just a little girl, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was a bright girl but... well, just careless. I was always leaving my books behind or forgetting my homework. I remember my third-grade teacher, Mrs. Higgins—she wore these beautiful pearl-rimmed cat-eye glasses—she constantly had to remind me to finish my seatwork before I was allowed to go outside for recess. \n\nRecess was just the best part of the day, though. We used to play double-dutch jump rope out on the blacktop. You just don't see children doing that anymore, do you? Now they're all just staring at those little screens. My grandson, Timmy, he's nine, and he just sits on the couch all afternoon playing that... what is it called... Minecraft? Just building little digital blocks. I keep telling my daughter she needs to send him outside for some fresh air. The weather has been so beautiful lately, hasn't it? The dogwoods in my front yard are finally blooming. I was actually thinking of cutting some branches to put in a nice crystal vase for the dining room... well, if I can ever get that table cleared off, I suppose. \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what were you asking me? Did I answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your memory or thinking getting worse recently?\n\nPatient: Worse recently? You know, I'm not sure if it's a new thing or just getting harder to manage, but I do seem to forget things all the time. It's just terrible. Like, I'll completely miss returning phone calls. And the bills... oh, I'll just leave a bill sitting right under a stack of mail and completely forget it's there. The mail we get these days is just out of control, isn't it? So many catalogs and flyers. I got one the other day from that outdoor company, and they had these lovely fleece-lined slippers. I actually thought about ordering a pair for my daughter, since her floors get so cold in the winter. She lives in one of those older split-level homes over in the historic district. Beautiful original hardwood floors, but oh, they are so drafty in January. \n\nBut anyway, I promised her I’d run some errands for her last week and it just completely slipped my mind. I felt awful about it. And it’s not just her... more than once lately, I’ve realized entirely too late that I never confirmed one of my husband’s appointments. The poor man is waiting to see his doctors, and I was supposed to call the office back to say yes, we want the Tuesday slot, but... well. I probably went to put the kettle on and just forgot. I love my whistling tea kettle, it’s a bright cherry red enamel. My sister got it for me for my birthday last year... or was it the year before? She always finds the best gifts at those little antique malls. \n\nI'm sorry, I lost my train of thought entirely. What were you asking me about? Was it about my husband's doctors?\n\n[Last Question]\nCompared with a few years ago, do you feel your thinking has gotten worse?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Compared with a few years ago? Well... no, I don't think it's actually gotten worse. It's really just the same as it's always been, you know? I've always been this way. Always losing my place, always getting distracted. Just like my mother, actually. She was the exact same way. Bless her heart, she could never keep a thought in her head for more than a minute. She used to walk into the kitchen and just stand there, staring at the refrigerator, trying to remember what she went in there for. 

Oh, she had this beautiful old Frigidaire, the kind with the rounded corners and the heavy chrome handle? They just don't make appliances like that anymore. Now you buy a refrigerator and it breaks in five years. And the repairmen charge an arm and a leg just to come look at it! My neighbor, Betty, she had a repairman out last month for her washing machine, and he charged her a hundred and fifty dollars just to tell her a belt was broken. Can you imagine? A hundred and fifty dollars! She was so upset, she just went and bought a new one at Sears. Well, I guess it wasn't Sears, they closed that store down at the mall, didn't they? It's such a shame, I used to love walking through the mall on a Saturday afternoon...

Oh, dear, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Something about my mother's refrigerator?

Down or depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I have my days where I feel a bit overwhelmed, sure, but depressed? No. I try to stay positive. I always say, you have to look on the bright side of things. That's what my grandmother used to tell me. She was such a cheerful woman, always baking something. The smell of fresh bread... oh, it was heavenly. She had this huge cast-iron stove that she cooked everything on. I don't know how she managed it, honestly. Those things get so hot, especially in the summer. 

We used to spend our summers at her house in the country. It was out past the county line, right near this little creek where my brother and I would catch tadpoles. We'd put them in a big glass mason jar and watch them grow legs. My brother, he was always getting into mischief. One time he tracked mud all through my grandmother's clean kitchen floor right after she'd scrubbed it on her hands and knees. She wasn't even mad, she just laughed and told him to go wash up at the pump outside. The water from that pump was always freezing cold, even in July. It came straight from an underground well, you know. They say well water is better for you than city water because of all the minerals. I read an article about that recently in a magazine I picked up at the dentist's office... 

Wait, what were we talking about? Was I telling you about my brother?

Work? Oh, well, I haven't worked in years, I retired a long time ago. But these problems... they do cause trouble, yes. I actually had to give up leading this big event at the senior center. It was a spring luncheon, and I was in charge of the catering and the decorations. We were going to have these beautiful centerpieces with yellow tulips and baby's breath. I just love yellow tulips, don't you? They remind me of my neighbor's garden when I was a little girl. She had this massive flower bed right along her white picket fence, and every spring it would just explode with color. She used to give me little cuttings to take home to my mother. My mother would put them in this delicate crystal vase she got for her wedding. It had this intricate etching on the side, little vines and leaves. I think my sister has that vase now. She always did love crystal. She has a whole cabinet full of it in her dining room... 

Anyway, it's gotten so bad that my daughter has started double-checking all my bills and appointments. She just doesn't trust that I can keep everything straight on my own anymore. It's a bit embarrassing, really. She comes over on Sundays and goes through my mail. We usually have tea while she does it. I make Earl Grey, it's her favorite. I buy the loose leaf kind from this little specialty shop downtown. The owner there is such a nice man, he always remembers my name and asks about my husband. He wears these funny little bow ties...

I was at the grocery store last week and a small box of treats was almost seven dollars. I just couldn't believe it. Everything is getting so terribly expensive, especially eggs. My husband loves his eggs over easy every morning, but at these prices... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, throwing things away. No, no, I don't hold onto useless things or anything like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been this easily distracted and disorganized since you were young?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, yes. It feels like it's been this way forever. Even when I was just a little girl, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was a bright girl but... well, just careless. I was always leaving my books behind or forgetting my homework. I remember my third-grade teacher, Mrs. Higgins—she wore these beautiful pearl-rimmed cat-eye glasses—she constantly had to remind me to finish my seatwork before I was allowed to go outside for recess. \n\nRecess was just the best part of the day, though. We used to play double-dutch jump rope out on the blacktop. You just don't see children doing that anymore, do you? Now they're all just staring at those little screens. My grandson, Timmy, he's nine, and he just sits on the couch all afternoon playing that... what is it called... Minecraft? Just building little digital blocks. I keep telling my daughter she needs to send him outside for some fresh air. The weather has been so beautiful lately, hasn't it? The dogwoods in my front yard are finally blooming. I was actually thinking of cutting some branches to put in a nice crystal vase for the dining room... well, if I can ever get that table cleared off, I suppose. \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what were you asking me? Did I answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your memory or thinking getting worse recently?\n\nPatient: Worse recently? You know, I'm not sure if it's a new thing or just getting harder to manage, but I do seem to forget things all the time. It's just terrible. Like, I'll completely miss returning phone calls. And the bills... oh, I'll just leave a bill sitting right under a stack of mail and completely forget it's there. The mail we get these days is just out of control, isn't it? So many catalogs and flyers. I got one the other day from that outdoor company, and they had these lovely fleece-lined slippers. I actually thought about ordering a pair for my daughter, since her floors get so cold in the winter. She lives in one of those older split-level homes over in the historic district. Beautiful original hardwood floors, but oh, they are so drafty in January. \n\nBut anyway, I promised her I’d run some errands for her last week and it just completely slipped my mind. I felt awful about it. And it’s not just her... more than once lately, I’ve realized entirely too late that I never confirmed one of my husband’s appointments. The poor man is waiting to see his doctors, and I was supposed to call the office back to say yes, we want the Tuesday slot, but... well. I probably went to put the kettle on and just forgot. I love my whistling tea kettle, it’s a bright cherry red enamel. My sister got it for me for my birthday last year... or was it the year before? She always finds the best gifts at those little antique malls. \n\nI'm sorry, I lost my train of thought entirely. What were you asking me about? Was it about my husband's doctors?\n\nDoctor: Compared with a few years ago, do you feel your thinking has gotten worse?\n\nPatient: Compared with a few years ago? Well... no, I don't think it's actually gotten worse. It's really just the same as it's always been, you know? I've always been this way. Always losing my place, always getting distracted. Just like my mother, actually. She was the exact same way. Bless her heart, she could never keep a thought in her head for more than a minute. She used to walk into the kitchen and just stand there, staring at the refrigerator, trying to remember what she went in there for. \n\nOh, she had this beautiful old Frigidaire, the kind with the rounded corners and the heavy chrome handle? They just don't make appliances like that anymore. Now you buy a refrigerator and it breaks in five years. And the repairmen charge an arm and a leg just to come look at it! My neighbor, Betty, she had a repairman out last month for her washing machine, and he charged her a hundred and fifty dollars just to tell her a belt was broken. Can you imagine? A hundred and fifty dollars! She was so upset, she just went and bought a new one at Sears. Well, I guess it wasn't Sears, they closed that store down at the mall, didn't they? It's such a shame, I used to love walking through the mall on a Saturday afternoon...\n\nOh, dear, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Something about my mother's refrigerator?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Down or depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I have my days where I feel a bit overwhelmed, sure, but depressed? No. I try to stay positive. I always say, you have to look on the bright side of things. That's what my grandmother used to tell me. She was such a cheerful woman, always baking something. The smell of fresh bread... oh, it was heavenly. She had this huge cast-iron stove that she cooked everything on. I don't know how she managed it, honestly. Those things get so hot, especially in the summer. \n\nWe used to spend our summers at her house in the country. It was out past the county line, right near this little creek where my brother and I would catch tadpoles. We'd put them in a big glass mason jar and watch them grow legs. My brother, he was always getting into mischief. One time he tracked mud all through my grandmother's clean kitchen floor right after she'd scrubbed it on her hands and knees. She wasn't even mad, she just laughed and told him to go wash up at the pump outside. The water from that pump was always freezing cold, even in July. It came straight from an underground well, you know. They say well water is better for you than city water because of all the minerals. I read an article about that recently in a magazine I picked up at the dentist's office... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Was I telling you about my brother?\n\nDoctor: Did these attention problems cause trouble for you at work?\n\nPatient: Work? Oh, well, I haven't worked in years, I retired a long time ago. But these problems... they do cause trouble, yes. I actually had to give up leading this big event at the senior center. It was a spring luncheon, and I was in charge of the catering and the decorations. We were going to have these beautiful centerpieces with yellow tulips and baby's breath. I just love yellow tulips, don't you? They remind me of my neighbor's garden when I was a little girl. She had this massive flower bed right along her white picket fence, and every spring it would just explode with color. She used to give me little cuttings to take home to my mother. My mother would put them in this delicate crystal vase she got for her wedding. It had this intricate etching on the side, little vines and leaves. I think my sister has that vase now. She always did love crystal. She has a whole cabinet full of it in her dining room... \n\nAnyway, it's gotten so bad that my daughter has started double-checking all my bills and appointments. She just doesn't trust that I can keep everything straight on my own anymore. It's a bit embarrassing, really. She comes over on Sundays and goes through my mail. We usually have tea while she does it. I make Earl Grey, it's her favorite. I buy the loose leaf kind from this little specialty shop downtown. The owner there is such a nice man, he always remembers my name and asks about my husband. He wears these funny little bow ties... \n\nOh, dear, I've wandered off again. What were we talking about? Was it the senior center luncheon?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Just like my mother, actually. She was the exact same way. Bless her heart, she could never keep a thought in her head for more than a minute. She used to walk into the kitchen and just stand there, staring at the refrigerator, trying to remember what she went in there for. \n\nOh, she had this beautiful old Frigidaire, the kind with the rounded corners and the heavy chrome handle? They just don't make appliances like that anymore. Now you buy a refrigerator and it breaks in five years. And the repairmen charge an arm and a leg just to come look at it! My neighbor, Betty, she had a repairman out last month for her washing machine, and he charged her a hundred and fifty dollars just to tell her a belt was broken. Can you imagine? A hundred and fifty dollars! She was so upset, she just went and bought a new one at Sears. Well, I guess it wasn't Sears, they closed that store down at the mall, didn't they? It's such a shame, I used to love walking through the mall on a Saturday afternoon...\n\nOh, dear, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Something about my mother's refrigerator?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Down or depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I have my days where I feel a bit overwhelmed, sure, but depressed? No. I try to stay positive. I always say, you have to look on the bright side of things. That's what my grandmother used to tell me. She was such a cheerful woman, always baking something. The smell of fresh bread... oh, it was heavenly. She had this huge cast-iron stove that she cooked everything on. I don't know how she managed it, honestly. Those things get so hot, especially in the summer. \n\nWe used to spend our summers at her house in the country. It was out past the county line, right near this little creek where my brother and I would catch tadpoles. We'd put them in a big glass mason jar and watch them grow legs. My brother, he was always getting into mischief. One time he tracked mud all through my grandmother's clean kitchen floor right after she'd scrubbed it on her hands and knees. She wasn't even mad, she just laughed and told him to go wash up at the pump outside. The water from that pump was always freezing cold, even in July. It came straight from an underground well, you know. They say well water is better for you than city water because of all the minerals. I read an article about that recently in a magazine I picked up at the dentist's office... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Was I telling you about my brother?\n\nDoctor: Did these attention problems cause trouble for you at work?\n\nPatient: Work? Oh, well, I haven't worked in years, I retired a long time ago. But these problems... they do cause trouble, yes. I actually had to give up leading this big event at the senior center. It was a spring luncheon, and I was in charge of the catering and the decorations. We were going to have these beautiful centerpieces with yellow tulips and baby's breath. I just love yellow tulips, don't you? They remind me of my neighbor's garden when I was a little girl. She had this massive flower bed right along her white picket fence, and every spring it would just explode with color. She used to give me little cuttings to take home to my mother. My mother would put them in this delicate crystal vase she got for her wedding. It had this intricate etching on the side, little vines and leaves. I think my sister has that vase now. She always did love crystal. She has a whole cabinet full of it in her dining room... \n\nAnyway, it's gotten so bad that my daughter has started double-checking all my bills and appointments. She just doesn't trust that I can keep everything straight on my own anymore. It's a bit embarrassing, really. She comes over on Sundays and goes through my mail. We usually have tea while she does it. I make Earl Grey, it's her favorite. I buy the loose leaf kind from this little specialty shop downtown. The owner there is such a nice man, he always remembers my name and asks about my husband. He wears these funny little bow ties... \n\nOh, dear, I've wandered off again. What were we talking about? Was it the senior center luncheon?\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any daily tasks you used to manage on your own that you can no longer do without help?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 70 / not_in_workforce / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Daily_Activities_Forgetfulness\nmanifestation: \"I’ve missed returning calls, left bills sitting under a stack of mail, forgotten errands I promised my daughter I’d do, and more than once realized too late that I never confirmed one of my husband’s appointments.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well, yes... I suppose there are quite a few things now. It's just silly things, really, but they add up. I've missed returning phone calls, and I keep leaving bills sitting under big stacks of mail until they're late. My daughter gets so frustrated because I'll completely forget errands I promised her I'd do. And my poor husband, more than once I've realized way too late that I never confirmed one of his appointments. 

He goes to that podiatrist over on 4th Street, Dr. Henderson. Such a nice man, but his waiting room is always absolutely freezing. They keep the air conditioning blasting even in November! I always have to bring a sweater. I knitted this lovely powder blue cardigan last winter just for that reason. It took me months because the yarn was so fine, but it turned out beautifully. I bought the yarn at this little craft fair they hold at the Methodist church every fall. They have the most wonderful bake sale there, too. Mrs. Gable makes these pecan pies that just melt in your mouth. She uses her grandmother's recipe, I think it calls for dark corn syrup instead of light, which gives it that rich flavor. I tried making it once for Thanksgiving, but I completely burnt the crust. The smoke alarm went off and the dog started howling... 

Oh my. I'm rambling again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What was the question? Something about my husband's doctor?

She had this massive flower bed right along her white picket fence, and every spring it would just explode with color. She used to give me little cuttings to take home to my mother. My mother would put them in this delicate crystal vase she got for her wedding. It had this intricate etching on the side, little vines and leaves. I think my sister has that vase now. She always did love crystal. She has a whole cabinet full of it in her dining room... \n\nAnyway, it's gotten so bad that my daughter has started double-checking all my bills and appointments. She just doesn't trust that I can keep everything straight on my own anymore. It's a bit embarrassing, really. She comes over on Sundays and goes through my mail. We usually have tea while she does it. I make Earl Grey, it's her favorite. I buy the loose leaf kind from this little specialty shop downtown. The owner there is such a nice man, he always remembers my name and asks about my husband. He wears these funny little bow ties... \n\nOh, dear, I've wandered off again. What were we talking about? Was it the senior center luncheon?\n\nDoctor: Are there any daily tasks you used to manage on your own that you can no longer do without help?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose there are quite a few things now. It's just silly things, really, but they add up. I've missed returning phone calls, and I keep leaving bills sitting under big stacks of mail until they're late. My daughter gets so frustrated because I'll completely forget errands I promised her I'd do. And my poor husband, more than once I've realized way too late that I never confirmed one of his appointments. \n\nHe goes to that podiatrist over on 4th Street, Dr. Henderson. Such a nice man, but his waiting room is always absolutely freezing. They keep the air conditioning blasting even in November! I always have to bring a sweater. I knitted this lovely powder blue cardigan last winter just for that reason. It took me months because the yarn was so fine, but it turned out beautifully. I bought the yarn at this little craft fair they hold at the Methodist church every fall. They have the most wonderful bake sale there, too. Mrs. Gable makes these pecan pies that just melt in your mouth. She uses her grandmother's recipe, I think it calls for dark corn syrup instead of light, which gives it that rich flavor. I tried making it once for Thanksgiving, but I completely burnt the crust. The smoke alarm went off and the dog started howling... \n\nOh my. I'm rambling again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What was the question? Something about my husband's doctor?"
  }

Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings). Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nOh, no, I don't have any trouble throwing things in the trash. I actually like getting rid of things when I know they're garbage. The problem is just sorting through the piles to figure out what's what. But once it's in the bin, it goes right out to the curb on Tuesday mornings. \n\nOur garbage man, Frank... oh, he is just the sweetest man. He’s been doing our neighborhood route for, gosh, it must be fifteen years now. He used to bring his golden retriever in the cab of the truck with him. Such a beautiful dog, her name was Daisy. I used to keep those little milk-bone biscuits in the pantry just in case I saw them out the window. Though, my goodness, have you seen the price of pet food lately? I was at the grocery store last week and a small box of treats was almost seven dollars. I just couldn't believe it. Everything is getting so terribly expensive, especially eggs. My husband loves his eggs over easy every morning, but at these prices... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, throwing things away. No, no, I don't hold onto useless things or anything like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"

Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment. She used to walk into the kitchen and just stand there, staring at the refrigerator, trying to remember what she went in there for. \n\nOh, she had this beautiful old Frigidaire, the kind with the rounded corners and the heavy chrome handle? They just don't make appliances like that anymore. Now you buy a refrigerator and it breaks in five years. And the repairmen charge an arm and a leg just to come look at it! My neighbor, Betty, she had a repairman out last month for her washing machine, and he charged her a hundred and fifty dollars just to tell her a belt was broken. Can you imagine? A hundred and fifty dollars! She was so upset, she just went and bought a new one at Sears. Well, I guess it wasn't Sears, they closed that store down at the mall, didn't they? It's such a shame, I used to love walking through the mall on a Saturday afternoon...\n\nOh, dear, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Something about my mother's refrigerator?\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
  }
]

  "confirmed": [
    "S008",
    "S021"
  ],
  "denied": [],

Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nDown or depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I have my days where I feel a bit overwhelmed, sure, but depressed? No. I try to stay positive. I always say, you have to look on the bright side of things. That's what my grandmother used to tell me. She was such a cheerful woman, always baking something. The smell of fresh bread... oh, it was heavenly. She had this huge cast-iron stove that she cooked everything on. I don't know how she managed it, honestly. Those things get so hot, especially in the summer. \n\nWe used to spend our summers at her house in the country. It was out past the county line, right near this little creek where my brother and I would catch tadpoles. We'd put them in a big glass mason jar and watch them grow legs. My brother, he was always getting into mischief. One time he tracked mud all through my grandmother's clean kitchen floor right after she'd scrubbed it on her hands and knees. She wasn't even mad, she just laughed and told him to go wash up at the pump outside. The water from that pump was always freezing cold, even in July. It came straight from an underground well, you know. They say well water is better for you than city water because of all the minerals. I read an article about that recently in a magazine I picked up at the dentist's office... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Was I telling you about my brother?\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
  }

Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected. I just love yellow tulips, don't you? They remind me of my neighbor's garden when I was a little girl. She had this massive flower bed right along her white picket fence, and every spring it would just explode with color. She used to give me little cuttings to take home to my mother. My mother would put them in this delicate crystal vase she got for her wedding. It had this intricate etching on the side, little vines and leaves. I think my sister has that vase now. She always did love crystal. She has a whole cabinet full of it in her dining room... \n\nAnyway, it's gotten so bad that my daughter has started double-checking all my bills and appointments. She just doesn't trust that I can keep everything straight on my own anymore. It's a bit embarrassing, really. She comes over on Sundays and goes through my mail. We usually have tea while she does it. I make Earl Grey, it's her favorite. I buy the loose leaf kind from this little specialty shop downtown. The owner there is such a nice man, he always remembers my name and asks about my husband. He wears these funny little bow ties... \n\nOh, dear, I've wandered off again. What were we talking about?
